The first line is the comment `: open`, so a client knows the stream is live before the first request ends. Each entry is a default (`message`) event: `id:` its sequence and `data:` one `LogTailEntry` JSON on one line (`sequence`, `time`, `clientAddress`, `host`, `matchedHost`, `method`, `path`, `query`, `protocol`, `status`, `responseBytes`, `totalMs`, `upstreamMs`, `upstream`, `requestIdentifier`, `cloudflareRay`, `referer`, `userAgent`; nulls omitted; the request ID is `requestIdentifier`, because the architecture rule keeps any `*Id` property of a contract a typed ID). A reader that falls behind gets `event: skipped` with `{"count":N}`; a comment `: heartbeat` goes out every 15 seconds of silence (`LogTailService.Heartbeat`, which a test shortens) so an idle proxy or client doesn't time the stream out and a vanished client fails the write. Query parameters: `host` (a configured host's name), `unmatched=true`, `statusClass` (`5` or `5xx`), `status`, `ip` (prefix), `method`, `path`, and `backlog` (0 to 1000, default 0, the newest matching entries first). Every bad parameter, an over-long text and `host` with `unmatched` are 400 problems; the 33rd stream is a 503 with `Retry-After: 5`. There is no maximum duration: the stream ends when the client disconnects (`RequestAborted`), which disposes the subscription (a test checks the hub's subscriber count returns to zero).
- **Tests.** bUnit (`LiveTailPageTests`, `LiveTailTextTests`, `SimulatorQueryTests`, 46): order and status colours, the empty state, a burst rendered in a handful of renders, pause and what waits, a hidden tab, every filter and the host picker, a bad status, the drawer with the request ID and the simulator link, a role without `logs:read` getting no subscription, disposal, the busy alert, the load test above, the status parser, the navigation entry. Playwright (`LiveTailEndToEndTests`): entries arrive over a real circuit, a filter and pause hold, a row opens its entry and leads to the simulator prefilled, the page fits 390 px.
- **Visual pass** (throwaway tour, not committed; dark, light and 390 px, with a 6 000-entry burst, a paused and filtered view, the empty state and the drawer) found and fixed: IPv6 clients overflowing their column into the next (ellipsis and a wider column), a hidden "Clear filters" button reserving a row of the filter card (it moved to the status line, shown only when a filter is set), the drawer's facts stretching to fill the drawer on a phone (the page's own list instead of the chip-style `.pdk-facts`), and the simulate button crowding the drawer's title (it moved to the footer).

**As built (M6): HSTS fields.** `HostSettingsFields` gained an **HSTS** group, shared by the global defaults, the profiles (and the profile drawer) and the host form, so an admin sets it once and every layer says where its value would come from. A switch ("Inherit (off)", On, Off) brings the rest with it: **Remembered for**, a select of 5 minutes (for testing), a day, six months, a year and two years plus custom seconds (`HstsMaxAgePresets`, kept as the number of seconds the config holds), **Every subdomain** and **Preload list**, each "Inherit (...)" with the inherited value in words. The copy says what a browser remembers and for how long, that a subdomain without a certificate stops working over plain HTTP, and that preload is hard to undo; the validator's errors show at the field they are about (the preload pair, the custom seconds). The group shows the rows that apply: inheriting "on" shows them, an explicit Off hides the preload pair, and **Remembered for** also shows when the layer sets an age with the switch off, so a number the validator refuses has a field to appear at. Cards and profile summaries say "HSTS: 1 year, subdomains, preload", "HSTS when on: ..." when the switch is left to the layer below, or "HSTS off".

**As built (M6): Server header fields.** The same form has a **Server header** group: a select with "Inherit (...)" naming the inherited mode in words (a custom value is named), then `Paduraksa (no version)`, `Custom value`, `The upstream's header` and `Remove`. A **Custom value** text field appears for the custom mode, and also when the layer already sets a value or the validator refuses one, so an error always has a field to appear at. The one helper line says that hiding the upstream's header stops it advertising its software and version; nothing repeats the label. Cards and profile summaries say "Server header: Paduraksa", "Server header: edge/1.0", "Server header: the upstream's header" or "Server header: none", and a value without a mode says "Server header value: ...".

**As built (M6): the settings fields bind to the contract.** `HostSettingsFields` works on the contract layer itself - a `HostSettings` where null means inherit - plus what that layer would inherit, and hands back a changed record with `SettingsChanged`; the form page owns it (the global defaults, a profile, a host), and there is no flat form model in between. The inherited values are `HostSettingsLayers.MergeAll` of the layers below over `BuiltIn`, so the form can't promise a default or a merge the proxy would not apply. Each field changes its own record with `with`, and `HostSettingsEdit.WithTls`/`WithHsts`/`WithServerHeader` collapses a nested record that sets nothing back to null by `ISettingsLayer.SetsNothing`, so clearing a field leaves no key behind. `SettingRow` is the row's title, help and control; `InheritSelect<T>` is a nullable value as a select whose first option says "Inherit (...)" - or "Built-in default (...)" on the global defaults - in the words of the value the layer below has, with the label, `data-field`, error and read-only state every field shares. HSTS's "Remembered for" keeps state of its own in a small component (`HstsMaxAgeField`: the preset-or-custom choice, derived from the stored seconds, with a custom number remembered while a preset is picked, as before; the IP allow list's own field went with the inline list in M7, which the access-list picker replaced). `SettingsSummary` holds one phrase per setting in the contract's order, so the cards and profile summaries need no per-field code elsewhere. The per-field `From`/`ApplyTo`/`Overrides` round trips are gone; `HostSettingsFieldsTests` is one table over every field - setting it through the control, the record that leaves, the inherited words, clearing it, and an error at its path - with a reflection completeness check over the contract, the dashboard's counterpart to `SettingsInheritanceTests`. The §5.3 "Adding a field to the settings UI" checklist is the whole cost of a new setting here.

**As built (M7): the security overview.** The first entry of the Security group (`/security`, `config:read`, "Security overview") answers "what is protecting my hosts, and is it working". `SecurityOverviewService` (`Paduraksa.Application.Security`, registered as a shared singleton at startup, so its `Since` is the process start) reads `ProofOfWorkStats`, `RateLimitStats` and `AccessListStats` (a card of its own under the proof-of-work rejections: refused by address, wrong or missing password, passwords checked, queued, refused busy) and, per enabled host, the *live compiled snapshot*, not the stored document: so it is what the gate is doing, and a revision the compiler rejected never shows. It needed three small additions to the compiled model: `CompiledHost.Names`, `CompiledHost.RateLimit` (the host's own policy object, which an inheriting route's chain shares, so a route whose `RateLimitPolicy` has another ID, or none, is an override) and `RateLimitPolicy.PolicyId`/`PolicyName`; nothing on the request path changed. A host reports proof of work (its switch and difficulty, plus how many routes differ, either way; Redirect/Respond/Admin hosts report it off, since the gate only runs on proxy routes), the rate-limit policy (with the routes that apply another policy or none, grouped), the robots.txt mode and its access list. The access list is read from `CompiledHost.AccessList` and `CompiledRoute.AccessList` (the compiled list each carries, which the host's and routes' chains no longer reveal once a host with routes on different lists holds only the address-only union), not from the policies in the chain. The column names the list, linked to its page, with what it asks of a visitor ("2 users" or "addresses only", from `CompiledAccessList.UserCount`); routes on another list, or none, are grouped like the rate-limit column's, so "Off on 2 routes" is a route that lifted it. `AccessListRef.AddressRanges` (merged ranges, `CidrSet.RangeCount`: `10.0.0.0/8` plus `10.1.0.0/16` is one range) rides along in the DTO for the API, the page does not print it. The overview is one immutable DTO (`Paduraksa.Contracts.Security.SecurityOverview`), read lock-free and stateless; `GET /api/v1/security/overview` returns it (`config:read`, in OpenAPI) for debugging with a token.

The page polls every 2 s with a `PeriodicTimer` (a `PollInterval` parameter for tests) and shows per-second rates computed from the difference between two samples over their `SampledAt` times (blank until the second sample). A small JS helper (`paduraksa.pageVisibility`) pauses polling while the tab is hidden and, on return, starts over from a fresh sample so a rate never spans the time away. Four compact tiles (challenges served; solved, with solve rate; rejected, the sum of bad solutions, non-browsers and the four cookie reasons; rate-limited, with its share of the requests a limit checked), the hosts table (protected hosts by name, then those with nothing on, each cell a muted "None" when empty; a stacked card per host on a phone), and the rejection reasons. No persistence and no background service: rollups are M9. **Gaps:** the permission is checked when the page opens, not on each tick (the session re-check still ends a revoked session's circuit); the rate-limit share counts only requests on routes with a policy; there is no per-host or per-route counter (the stats are process-wide).

**As built (`analytics`): the Analytics page.** **Analytics** is the first entry under Insight (route `/analytics`, permission `metrics:read`, which every role holds, so the page is for everyone who can read the overview). It reads `AnalyticsService` (Application) directly, as the home overview reads `MetricsService`, and the §7.4 "As built: traffic analytics" has the numbers behind it. The header holds a **host picker** (all hosts, each configured host by its first name when the role may read the config, and "No host matched") and the 1 h / 24 h / 7 d / 30 d switch of the overview (default 24 h), and one **"approximate" note** whose tooltip says once what the sketches mean (about 1.6 % for visitors, a count marked with the approximation sign may be higher than the truth by at most what its own tooltip says, and a path's p95 covers the requests since it entered the table); nothing else on the page explains itself.
- **Top.** Four tiles: requests (with the share from bots), bandwidth ("declared sizes only": a streamed body declares none), unique visitors (marked with the approximation sign) and the error rate (5xx). Nothing is drawn for a range with no requests: one empty state says that a host with its access log off is never counted.
- **Visitors and content.** Three charts (requests with a 5xx line, bytes sent, unique visitors), the overview's inline-SVG `TimeSeriesChart`, no dependency: **a step nobody observed is a shaded gap and its line breaks, an observed quiet step is a real zero** (the M9 rule, from the service's `coveredSeconds`). Then bar lists with shares (`ShareList`: a label, a bar as wide as the share, the count, the percent): status classes (green, blue, amber and red by class) and the top exact codes, methods and HTTP versions; then tables: top paths (requests, bandwidth, p95, errors), top referers, slowest paths, largest responses. A count a sketch may have overshot is written with the approximation sign and a tooltip "Between X and Y". A path is a link to the **Live tail filtered to it** (and to the host when the page is for one; `/logs/live?path=...&host=...`); a path the analytics cut at their cap is plain text. The Live tail learned `?path=` and `?host=` for this (a host the picker does not offer is ignored, a path is cut at the filter's cap). **A user agent is not a link**: the tail has no user-agent filter, and adding one would reach into the tail's contract for little.
- **Clients and bots.** Browsers (families, with versions in the API), operating systems and devices as bar lists with shares; bots by kind; the top bots with their kind; the bots ignoring robots.txt (its empty state says only hosts that serve a robots.txt are checked: the one consequence a person needs to act on); and the raw user agents. The overview's Traffic card links to the page.
- **Updates.** The page reads every 10 s while the tab is visible (`paduraksa.pageVisibility`, as the overview) and the longer ranges six times less often; the service keeps a result for 5 s (30 s beyond the hour). A range that cannot be read is an alert with "Try again".
- **Tests.** bUnit (`AnalyticsPageTests`, 14, over a real database): the empty state, the tiles, the three charts and a gap, the breakdowns with their tones and shares, the tables with links and approximation marks, the clients and bots, the ignoring-robots empty state, the one tooltip, the range and host switches (the blog's own name in the tail link), a cut path, a database that is down and recovers, the navigation entry; `LiveTailPageTests` for the query parameters. Playwright (`AnalyticsEndToEndTests`): the page over a real circuit with seeded buckets and a gap, a path leading to the filtered Live tail, the 7 d switch, and 390 px without sideways scroll. **Visual pass** (throwaway tour in dark, light and 390 px) found and fixed: the top-paths table cut off in a three-column grid (the table cards are wider), bar labels truncated ("Search en..."), and the header's actions overflowing at 390 px (they wrap).

**Planned: drafts (change sets across pages).** Today every edit is its own review and its own revision; a host drawer can
stage a new upstream or profile, but only inside that one drawer. A **draft** collects edits made anywhere (hosts,
upstreams, profiles, rate limits, access lists, global defaults, a route's settings) and applies them as **one revision**
when the admin is ready, so a change that spans several pages goes live at once or not at all.
- **Where edits go.** While a draft is open, a form's primary button is "Add to draft" (Review and Apply now stay
  available as the secondary path). The edit is checked as it is added (the same dry run and inline errors as today) but
  nothing is committed. A bar across the dashboard shows the open draft: how many changes, "Review", "Discard".
- **What a draft is.** The *commands* (`UpsertHostCommand`, `DeleteUpstreamCommand`, ...) in the order they were added,
  plus the revision it was started on; not a frozen document. Each page reads "the head with the draft applied", so a
  host form shows an upstream the draft adds, and pickers offer it marked "Draft". Editing the same object twice keeps
  one entry (the later edit replaces the earlier), and an entry can be removed from the review on its own.
- **Stored server-side** in the config database, per user, one open draft each: it survives a reload, a second tab and a
  restart, and nothing in it is live or audited until it is applied. A draft untouched for 30 days is dropped (with a
  notice the next time its owner signs in). Secrets typed into a draft (an access-list password, a DNS token) are not
  stored in the draft row: they go to the vault as pending values bound to the draft and are dropped with it.
- **Review and apply.** "Review" replays the commands on the current head and shows the existing review panel over the
  whole set: one diff grouped by object, the compiler's warnings, the lock-out guard, and "Simulate" against the draft
  (the simulator already takes a draft document). Apply commits one revision (message defaults to a summary of the
  entries, editable), audited once with every entry listed; Undo on it undoes the whole draft.
- **Someone changed the config meanwhile.** Replaying commands on the new head is the merge: an entry whose object
  changed elsewhere since it was added (version check, as an object command does today) is marked "Changed elsewhere"
  with Reload-this-entry, and the rest still apply; the draft never silently overwrites another admin's change.
- **Not drafted:** what isn't config. Certificates, ACME accounts, DNS providers, users and tokens act at once as today
  (a draft can reference a certificate created meanwhile).
- **API:** `/api/v1/drafts` (create, list own, add/replace/remove an entry, review as a dry run, apply, discard), behind
  `config:write`; `If-Match` on apply carries the draft's version. MCP gets the same (M10), which suits agents that want
  to propose several changes for a human to apply.

**As built (`notify-outbox`): notification history.** **Settings, Notification history** (`/settings/notifications/history`, `providers:read`) and the delivery figures on the channel list and in the channel drawer are described with the outbox in §7.4 "As built: durable delivery and history": it sits under Settings, not Insight, because it is read and retried with the channels' permissions and linked from the channel list.

**As built: dashboard conventions (UI consistency pass).** Shared pieces first; a page that needs its own look for one of these is
a sign the shared piece is missing something.

- **`SegmentedControl<T>`** is the one single-choice-among-a-few control (time ranges, Mine/Everyone's, a host's kind, a token's
  expiry, the certificate filter). Options are `SegmentOption<T>` (value, label, optional icon, tooltip, `data-value` key, count,
  tone dot). It is a `radiogroup` of `radio` buttons: the chosen segment is the one tab stop, the arrow keys, Home and End move
  the choice (it follows focus, as native radios do, and wraps), and `paduraksa.js` keeps the page from scrolling on those keys.
  It is always one 40 px row beside a dense outlined field, the chosen segment raised; when the segments don't fit (a phone) it
  fills the width and wraps onto further rows rather than hiding options behind a scroll. Tests find it by
  `[data-field=...] [data-value=...]` and read `aria-checked`. A choice whose options need a sentence each is not a segment: it is
  `ChoiceCards` (below). A segment with a zero count is muted, or left out when it is a filter.
- **Page header:** the title (h1) and the actions share one centre line in a row at least 40 px tall, so a title sits at the same
  height on every page; a lead, only where it says something the page doesn't, sits under the row.
- **Headings:** a card's title is h6 (`Typo.h6`); a card under a section heading, on a page that has several (Analytics), is
  `subtitle1`; the section heading itself is h6 with `.pdk-section-heading`. A small label inside a form is `subtitle2`.
- **Filter cards** (Audit, Live tail) are `.pdk-filters`: an optional bar (a segmented control, "Clear filters" at the end), then
  the fields in one wrapping grid; every field is dense outlined and floats its label (give it a placeholder).
- **Heights and sizes:** dense outlined fields and the segmented control are 40 px; icon buttons are 36 px (`Size.Small`, never
  `Medium`) and carry a tooltip or `aria-label`; a drawer's header is at least 80 px whether or not it has a subtitle.
- **Buttons:** one filled primary action per view, outlined for a secondary action that stands alone, text for the rest, destructive
  actions in the error colour. **A list page shows its primary action once:** while the list is empty the empty state carries it (the
  same size as the header's, `Size.Small` only inside a compact card) and the page header hides its own (`PageHeader.ListEmpty`); once
  the list has items the header has it. Both placements keep the one `data-action` hook (and any class a test uses), so a test clicks the
  same selector either way. An empty state that offers a different, secondary action (History's "Add an upstream") is outlined.
- **Labels:** every outlined text, numeric, select and autocomplete field floats its label on the border, empty or not, so forms never
  mix floated and resting labels and a row lines up. MudBlazor floats it only for a field with a value or a placeholder and has no
  global default for `ShrinkLabel` (nor a theme or cascading option), so `app.css` applies MudBlazor's own shrink rules (the label's
  transform, the border's notch) to every outlined field, with no call site setting anything. A field with a placeholder shows it
  (give a field one where an example helps); the label never covers it. The dense toolbar and filter fields are unchanged in height.
  `FormLabelsEndToEndTests` holds an empty and a filled field's label to one position.
- **Choices with explanations** (`ChoiceCards<T>`, options are `ChoiceOption<T>`: title, one line of help, optional icon): Import's merge
  or replace, Global defaults' unknown-host answer and client addresses, the ACME challenge, the DNS provider and the notification
  service. One look: cards in a wrapping grid (at least 190 px each), the title and its help under it, the icon level with the title,
  the chosen card outlined and tinted in the primary colour; a radiogroup with the segmented control's keyboard behaviour. There is no
  MudRadio list in the dashboard. The help sits on the option, so the setting's own help line does not say it again. Tests find a card by
  `[data-field=...] [data-value=...]`.
- **Text a person copies** is `CopyableCode`: the text in one box with the copy control inside its top-right corner, so all such
  values share one left and right edge. `SecretValue` (shown once) wraps it with a dashed, tinted box; the MCP snippets, the
  local CA fingerprint, the TOTP key and the restore command use it too. Recovery codes keep their Copy and Download pair.
- **Pills:** `.pdk-chip` and `.pdk-pill` are status (one look, tone by meaning: success, warning, error, info, default);
  `.pdk-badge` is the smaller label or count; `.pdk-tag` is a neutral tag. A row that summarises and filters is a segmented
  control with counts, never a row of pills; a row that only summarises is stat tiles.
- **Share-bar lists** (`ShareList`: status codes, methods, browsers, bots by kind and the rest) are one grid with a subgrid per row:
  the label, bar, count and percentage columns are as wide as the widest row needs, so every bar starts and ends at the same x; a tiny
  share is at least 3 px of fill from the track's start; counts and percentages are tabular and right-aligned.
- **Units:** a compact control writes units short ("1 h", "24 h", "7 d", "30 d", "1 y"), with the long form as its tooltip;
  prose writes them out ("7 days").
- **The connection snippets** of an AI assistant (`McpConnect`) take the token just issued and fill it into the commands: the
  token drawer shows them open, with the token only in that component's parameter and the markup built from it (it goes with the
  drawer, as the token's own box does), and says how to turn MCP on when it is off.

### 12.1 First-run setup
Security: until setup completes, the admin app serves **only** the wizard. That requires a one-time **setup token** printed to the logs and the console (`paduraksa setup-token` reprints it), which stops anyone else on the LAN from claiming the instance.

1. Setup token → 2. **Owner account** (+ optional passkey/TOTP) → 3. **Admin access** (port, bind, allowed CIDRs, admin hostname) → 4. **ACME account** (email, CA, ToS) → 5. **DNS provider** (pick, enter creds → live *Test*) → 6. **Wildcard certificate** (`example.com` + `*.example.com`, with live progress through order → TXT → propagation → issued) → 7. **Global defaults** (security preset: *Off / Balanced / Strict*, HSTS, default-host behaviour, robots preset) → 8. **First host** (optional, with an upstream reachability test) → Done (summary + "export config" + backup reminder).

Each step is skippable where it makes sense (e.g., HTTP-01 only, no DNS provider), and the wizard can be resumed.

**As built (M4).** Setup is incomplete while no Owner exists. At startup (the core host, after the config store) the server writes a 256-bit setup token to `<data dir>/setup-token` (mode 0600; a copy readable by others is discarded and replaced), logs it at Warning on **one line** (the console logger is single-line, so a multi-line message would be flattened) and prints **one boxed banner** to standard output, which `docker logs` captures (the box sized to its longest line, so the borders always line up; `+`, `-` and `|` when the console's output encoding is not UTF-8) and deletes the file once an Owner exists; `paduraksa setup-token` prints the same banner, or says setup is complete. `SetupService.CompleteSetupAsync(token, username, password, displayName)` compares the token in constant time, then checks that no Owner exists and creates the Owner in one write transaction (two simultaneous attempts make one), audits it, and deletes the token. A wrong token is audited. `SetupService.IsCompleteAsync` is what the admin app asks. CLI: `admin reset-password <user>` reads the password from standard input or a no-echo prompt (never argv), ends the user's sessions and clears a lockout; `admin disable-2fa <user>` turns the second factor off; both are audited as the `cli` actor.

**As built (M5): the wizard (trimmed).** Steps: setup token → Owner → two-step sign-in → ACME account → DNS provider → certificate → first host → done (admin access and global defaults wait for their pages). `SetupWizard` (dashboard) is the step list, the addresses and the way through; `WizardStepper` is the rail, plain HTML so the static setup page shows it too.
- **Token and Owner** stay the static form (`/setup`, posting to `/auth/setup`) with its rules unchanged: refused through the proxy, the setup token required, the gate open only to it while no Owner exists. It gains the rail and a password strength hint (`setup.js`, a classic script from this origin; the form works without it). On success the form endpoint now sends the new Owner to `AdminWebOptions.SetupCompletedPage` (`/welcome`) instead of the home page.
- **The rest is `/welcome/{step}`**, an ordinary signed-in page (the step parsed at the edge; an unknown one shows "no such step"). Each step uses the normal services as the signed-in user, so permissions and audit apply as anywhere else: **two-step sign-in** (`TwoFactorService`: the password again, then the QR code of the `otpauth://` URI drawn as inline SVG by `QrCode`, a byte-mode level-M encoder of our own checked with zbarimg for every version, with the key shown for typing; a code turns it on and the ten recovery codes show once; the URI and the codes are dropped from the component as the step moves on), **ACME account** and **DNS provider** (the settings forms; a new provider is tested at once; "Skip: HTTP-01 only"), **certificate** (from the ACME CA with live progress, a domain's wildcard proposed when there is a DNS provider, or from the local CA, the fallback offered when an ACME run fails), **first host** (a name, proposed from the certificate, and an upstream URL; "Check" runs `UpstreamProbe` (Application): a TCP connection within 3 s to the URL's host and port, closed at once, nothing sent or read, reachable or not, `config:write` required and a refusal audited; then the upstream and the host go through the config pages' review drawer as one `ReplaceConfigCommand` revision) and **done** (what is set up and what was skipped, links, and the trust-the-root card). Every step after the Owner can be skipped; Continue needs it done.
- **Resumable.** What is done is read from the data, not remembered (`SetupProgress`: the user's second factor, an active ACME account, a DNS provider, a certificate other than the admin's and the root, a host), so the wizard opens anywhere. The overview shows the open steps (the DNS provider only once there is an ACME account; what the user can't read isn't listed) as a "Finish setting up" checklist with links back into the wizard, until they are done or it is dismissed (remembered in the browser's local storage by `setup-checklist.js`, a module from this origin).
- **Not done:** the admin-access and global-defaults steps (the defaults have their settings page), passkeys, "export config" (M10) and the backup reminder (a backup can be made from Settings > Backup, §8.4, but the checklist does not ask for one). The two-step step is `TwoStepEnrollment`, shared with My account since the settings pages: it goes through `IDashboardIdentity`, so its password check now takes one of the API's derivation slots. Continue on the certificate step is enabled as soon as the certificate is issued (it used to stay disabled until the progress had been read again).
- **Tests.** bUnit: the upload, ACME account and DNS provider forms (validation, secrets cleared, write-only), the test verdicts, the progress list per state, the wizard's addresses, order, rail and checklist. Playwright (`SetupWizardEndToEndTests`): token → Owner → TOTP through the QR step → ACME and DNS skipped → local-CA certificate → first host to a test upstream (checked, reviewed, applied) → done; the certificate listed with its host; a PEM (made by openssl, skipped without it) uploaded; a DNS provider added and tested (no fake is reachable through the vault's handler, so the verdict is the real API's or a network failure; the test checks that a verdict shows and the token doesn't); the overview's checklist; screenshots of every step with `PADURAKSA_E2E_SCREENSHOTS`.

**Owner decision (post-M5): no wizard.** The step-by-step wizard after the Owner was more ceremony than help, so it is gone (`/welcome`, `SetupWizard`, `WizardStepper` and the certificate, first-host and summary steps). The setup page is still the one static form (token, then Owner) and its rules are unchanged; on success it sends the Owner to the overview (`SetupCompletedPage` is the home page). Everything else is done on its own page, when the person wants: the overview's "Finish setting up" checklist (`SetupChecklist`, from `SetupProgress`, read from the data as before) lists the open tasks (`SetupTasks`: two-step sign-in, an ACME account, a DNS provider once there is an account, a certificate, a first host), each linking to the page that does it (My account, ACME accounts, DNS providers, Certificates, New host), until they are done or it is dismissed. Two-step enrolment (`TwoStepEnrollment`) lives on in My account; the settings forms and the certificate drawer were already shared. Tests: bUnit for the checklist's tasks and links; Playwright (`FirstRunEndToEndTests`): token → Owner → the overview with its checklist → two-step from My account through the QR step → a local-CA certificate and an uploaded PEM from the certificates page → the checklist with only what is still open.

## 13. Risks & spikes (do these first)

| # | Spike | Why | Exit criterion |
|---|---|---|---|
| S1 ✅ *(partial: browser upgrade pending)* | **HTTP/3 + per-SNI cert selection** | Unverified whether Kestrel's QUIC listener invokes per-SNI selection. libmsquic availability in the container. | Either per-SNI works on QUIC with hot-swap, or the single-H3-cert fallback (§7.5) is implemented. **Plus:** the browser actually upgrades to H3 in the Docker deployment (`Alt-Svc` carries the public port, UDP 443 reachable). |
| S2 ✅ *(design changed: gate selects the endpoint)* | **YARP config churn + metadata binding** | Many hosts (500+) and frequent publishes: endpoint rebuild cost. `ConfigureEndpoints(Action<IEndpointConventionBuilder, RouteConfig>)` exists in YARP 2.3.0, but we need to confirm it re-runs on every dynamic reload with the new `RouteConfig`. | Publish < 50 ms at 500 hosts; no request errors during swaps under load; typed metadata on every endpoint matches its generation (or the route-ID fallback in §6.2 is in place). |
| S3 ✅ *(0 B gate; see §7.1)* | **Zero-alloc gate on Kestrel** | Request-scoped state without `Items`, span-based host lookup, feature reuse per connection. | BenchmarkDotNet + in-process test shows 0 B/request from Paduraksa code. |
| S4 ✅ *(partial: `FrozenDictionary` lookup not covered)* | **Typed IDs x EF Core 10 x SQLite** | Convention converters, BLOB big-endian GUIDs (`UuidV7Generator` + `TryWriteBytes(span, bigEndian: true, out _)`), JSON complex types containing typed IDs, LINQ translation of comparisons, `FrozenDictionary` `OrdinalIgnoreCase` alternate span lookup. | Round-trip, query and index-ordering tests pass. **Result:** done as real code in `Paduraksa.Data` (the generic parts moved to `Paduraksa.Storage` in M3) (`tests/Paduraksa.Tests.Data`, real SQLite). Everything worked as planned: BLOB/INTEGER/JSON-string storage, `ORDER BY` = `CompareTo` = string order (same millisecond too), comparisons translate directly, and an Int64Ordered key is the rowid alias without `AUTOINCREMENT`. Changes: discovery replaces EF's `PropertyDiscoveryConvention` instead of scanning (§4); JSON stores strings. |
| S5 ✅ *(implemented as the real host)* | **Two WebApplications, one process** | Shared singletons, graceful shutdown order, logging, health. | Both apps start/stop cleanly; integration tests can host both. |
| S6 ✅ *(partial: YARP health and rate-limit continuity tested; (b) with M9)* | **State continuity across publishes** (§6.3) | Rate limits and metrics must not reset on config edits. | Load test with publishes every 100 ms shows (a) continuous 429 behaviour, (b) monotonic counters, and (c) **YARP destination health preserved**: a destination marked unhealthy (active or passive) stays unhealthy across reloads that don't touch its cluster, and no healthy destination flaps. **M1** (`StateContinuityTests`): no request errors and (c), over real YARP reloads, policy-only edits and unchanged configs. **M7** (`RateLimitContinuityTests`): (a), buckets carried over publishes under load (the 2xx count stays within burst + rate x elapsed and 429s keep coming), plus a lowered burst taking effect on the next touch. (b) is tested with the metric slots (M9). |
| S7 ✅ *(baseline recorded; harness covers HAProxy, Traefik, Envoy and bare YARP, and keeps a run history; tail stalls under investigation)* | **Baseline perf vs nginx/Caddy** | Know where we stand early. | `oha`/`wrk2` numbers on the same box recorded in `bench/README.md`. **Result** (`bench/results/2026-11-07-baseline.md`, raw data alongside; current tables in `bench/RESULTS.md`): Paduraksa reaches ≈0.54-0.71x nginx's throughput and 2.1-3.6x Caddy's; at fixed rates its median latency is within 0.15 ms of nginx's but its p99 is 1.4-2.3x nginx's, from process-wide stalls that aren't GC-driven and are still under investigation. The harness now also runs HAProxy, Traefik, Envoy and a bare Kestrel + YARP reference, adds `body-64k` and `handshake` scenarios, and records run-to-run history with `RECORD=1`. |
